I tend to put together structures that are harmonised and meaningful in such a way that, on the basis of the small structures and the control by the contributors and by the insured community, one has the best possible success to get the money for health care and pension services and not for the administration.

ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-11-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00111_d7e7000	Mr President. Federal Chancellor! Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! We are discussing the new university law today. In recent years, the National Council has been more often confronted with university reforms. This is partly also a problem, because if universities are reformed permanently, then that means that those who work at universities have to spend a lot of time on self-employment and that energy is not directed towards what the universities actually have to do to take care of teaching and research. That is why, I believe, there is also a danger in the temptation to reform the universities constantly, because this is diverted from the actual task. I am afraid that this will also be the case with this law, ladies and gentlemen! [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] But I mean that it is not to be disputed that we have some problems in the field of universities to solve. It is still true that Austria has a relatively low academic rate in international comparison. Unfortunately, despite women's promotion, there are too few women in professor positions at the Austrian universities. Unfortunately, it is still that the drop-out rates are relatively high and that there are a number of difficulties for students in the course of study, for example, in waiting for exam dates and the range of laboratory places. There are a number of practical questions to be solved. Which is perhaps the most obvious thing, that's my own experience. I remember studying at the University of Vienna at the beginning of the 1980s, and at that time there were 500 students at our institute. Last year I visited "my" institute again, and the number of teachers, professors and assistants there was identical to that 20 years ago, but the number of students has increased tenfold in the meantime. For this reason, ladies and gentlemen, there is of course also a problem in relation to how many teachers are available at universities and how many students are available at universities. Therefore, we are entitled to say that we need a certain amount of action at the universities, even if the services provided by our universities can withstand the highest level and an assessment internationally. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] I say this, above all, because in every reform debate there is always a tendency to exaggerate and those who are implementing a reform must always do so or do so as if the current situation was a particularly bad one. From the description of the current state and the so-called alternative of the world-class university, the impression is to be given that the university is now being transferred to an optimal state with a reform step from the worst possible state. I believe that this is not a fair assessment, because it must be said quite openly that both in individual areas of research and, above all, in teaching, what Austrian universities do in their entirety is quite respectable and tangible, and that the graduates of Austrian universities find excellent employment opportunities both in Austria and in the international context. All six experts did not consider this to be a useful step. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] This example also makes clear – that is the fear that many have – that there is a hidden agenda behind this university reform, that there is not a question of new medical universities, because one considers this to be relevant, but because some want the number of management functions to be increased. ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-11-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00111_d7e7216	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! High house! Dear guests, especially guests of honor! I am delighted to welcome the chairman of the Rectors' Conference Magnificence Winckler and the Reform Editor Leopold March and all other guests, including from the field of art universities and the ÖH. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP]] Ladies and gentlemen! We are facing the conclusion of a major reform work today. A large Austrian daily has summarized: A similar change was made 153 years ago. What happens to this reform work? – The Austrian universities stop being dependent, subordinate departments of the Ministry. They are no longer "ministerial universities", but self-employed educational institutions, who are able to enter into service agreements on the basis of three-year global budgets and employ their own staff in accordance with their own employee's right – including the possibility of a pension fund. The university's management is also modernised: The rectorate – similar to a board of directors, but just like it – the university council – similar to a Supervisory Board – and the Senate – similar to the academic Parliament – will in future lead the new independent university in an efficient way in a balanced division of tasks and in a balanced relationship of power: on the basis of the Austrian federal constitution. This reform has become necessary because in recent years and decades universities have developed into inconspicuous, stately centralist large businesses, in which the bodies have increased more and more – up to 200 and more were alone at the University of Vienna – but the decisions have become increasingly opaque and incomprehensible. The liberation illusion of the 68-iger movement corresponded to a body- and state-organised group university, which often paralyzed itself and then made the ministry responsible for it in non-function. It was very comfortable. We want to end this convenient but dishonest way! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] Today there are about twice as many students and teachers compared to the 1960s. No business, no business in the economy would say that you can work with the same or with the same structures in a twice-sized company. Small adjustments are not sufficient to take account of these major changes. There were minor changes in 1993 with the UOG and the associated partial autonomy. It was just a part of autonomy and not the necessary autonomy that we are now making possible by law 2002. There were still overregulation, superimposition of monocratic and democratic professional decisions, intransparency and inefficiency, and the students sometimes felt that they were more invited to stay than to study quickly and efficiently. I am very grateful to Mrs Gehrer for her courageous and determined determination in relation to the 2000 government programme: If we want to implement something, we must continue this reform and bring it into a final form. We must give universities full legal and business capacity! – It is thanks to you, Mrs Federal Minister, that this decision has been incorporated into the government programme and we are facing the adoption of this reform work today. Throughout Europe, ladies and gentlemen, universities are being reformed. This ranges from the north-eastern neighboring states to the west. These are the key factors for the improved performance of the universities. At this point we have on 15 November. It was very important to me, however, that we do not water down the pillars on which we have built this university reform. These pillars are: The universities become legal persons of public law and no GmbH. It is a fundamental pillar for me that we ensure the overall responsibility of the state through a dynamic budget in the law. It is another pillar for me that we work with a slim administration in the universities, where there is strategic, scientific and operational separation of power. I therefore ask you not to use the word "polite commissioners" any more. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] Other key cornerstones of this university reform: the combination of decision-making and responsibility, the consistent implementation of the new service right, which was decided here, the way from an order culture to a culture of responsibility, the new forms of participation and the continuous assessment of teaching offers by students, which must also follow the consequences. These principles, these pillars, are the structure of this law, and we have received these pillars. All around, we have implemented many good developments that have been brought in by you in the law. With these pillars and with this law, we are in line with what is required by future-oriented universities at European level, namely: more autonomy – which does not mean everyone does what he wants, laisser-fair, but this autonomy is a responsible freedom to the state and society – more personal responsibility instead of a body university, a division of powers as we have done, and the subsidiarity, the delivery of responsibility to subordinate bodies. Ladies and gentlemen! It is particularly important to me to bear in mind the following: The university law clearly shows the responsibility for the entire society of the universities in our country. ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-11-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00111_d7e12945	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! High house! Dr. Martin Graf: The Firnberg recording exam!]] I am aware that some art universities, including those who are closest to me as a founder, the University of Music and Performing Arts Graz, have wished for implementation at a later date. In the end, however, I find the arguments for this less convincing than the ones against it. For example: In an article in today's edition of the "Presse" I find the desire to first complete the UOG 93, because it was only in 1997 that it was possible to start implementation. – This is understandable, and I also admit that this is not particularly funny in the execution, but we are honest: Prolongation of a move and delay in such an important reform would be preprogrammed. A closer look at the benefits of a simultaneous reform for all universities clearly outweighs the benefits. The first and most important advantage is: It is necessary for profile formation to go to the budget negotiations at the same time. In this context, I would like to focus briefly on the comments made by Mrs Glavischnig, who expressed her fears about the quality of artistic education and the fear that the trend towards the market economy has been moving. I think these fears are really unnecessary in this discussion, they are just unnecessarily emotionalizing. It is about creating framework conditions with the UOG 2002. The autonomous university determines its contents, its goodness, its class itself. At the same time, because otherwise, there is a dependence and imbalances between the universities. These could not be offset in a long time. At the same time, however, there is participation in the draft regulation on the indicator-specific budget. A one year later introduction would only allow corrections for the universities of the arts to be made to a regulation already adopted. Secondly, It is not to be expected that a year in old law will bring a more experience. This has even been repeated in practice by rectors of the art universities. That would be just a breathing break, a delay, in turn a push. Thirdly, as an argument from the other universities, the fact that, due to its size or its small size and its internal structure, the art universities are more predestined to pass into the new law more quickly, because they are more manageable and flexible and their number is even lower. Fourthly, study law has not been changed so much that major conversion problems could arise. Example: The implementation of the KUOG 1998, the UniStG 1997 and the Novelle 1999, which went very quickly at all art universities. Summing up once again: The universities of the arts should not be treated differently, they should not go after others, they should not be disadvantaged at all, because they should be able to benefit from the benefits of full law as early as possible: a year earlier global budget and more scope for action, full organisational autonomy, full personal autonomy. ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-11-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00111_d7e13627	Mr President. Thank you, Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen, With this paper we are discussing here today, we liberate the universities from a heavy yoke. We free the universities from excessive state influence and flaw, we free the universities from the yoke of bureaucratic barriers and rigid, inflexible structures, and we ultimately also free the universities from the yoke of a chamberalistic and inflexible financing system. But we not only liberate universities in the sense of demands that have been raised very often in recent years – including within universities – but we bring the universities with this reform and with this reform law very much. We give universities independence and autonomy as well as the opportunity to make independent and self-determined decisions, as was often demanded in recent years within and outside the universities. We give universities the opportunity to define their own priorities and their future development and thus also to insist on increased international and national competition from various training and education institutions and to take top positions. However, contrary to many statements in the course of the discussion on this university reform and also in the House today, the liberation from the state influence on autonomous decisions does not mean that the state is also stealing from its financial responsibility for the universities, for teaching and research in Austria. Rather, it is said that the state that the Republic of Austria expressly acknowledges its financial responsibility for the universities with this university law! The debated draft provides for the federal government to finance universities, and such an explicit legal financing guarantee is something new. But the financial responsibility of the Republic is not only established in this Reform Act in principle, but also the financial responsibility is determined and secured according to the level and guarantees universities at the current high level fixed budgets, including valorizations, including the tuition fees and all savings that will be possible for universities through this Reform Act. In other words, ladies and gentlemen, this new university law provides universities with even new additional financial scope. In the run-up to this university law, a great deal was still being moved in the negotiations with the rector's conference and with its chairman, Mr. Rector Winckler, who is also an economist. Many things have also been changed in the sense of the rector's conference. The fact that a result could be achieved by agreement shows that universities need not fear the future in financial terms, but rather that they will drive better and at the same time have the opportunity to set and use their financial resources much more autonomously and autonomously and thus be more efficient. Thank you.

My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! There are a large number of funding facilities that are currently less clear and less transparent. There are few cooperation networks, especially with international research institutions. In addition, many of these programmes are under-valued – only 20 percent of the total research programmes are evaluated for their efficiency at all – which, of course, does not lead to the use of research funding having the desired leverage effect. This is an Austrian specification that we have to change. I am thinking only of extra-university research within the framework of our house, BMVIT: We currently have seven societies, 14 sub-companies, countless participations in the countries, many centres of competence, into which we also bring our impulse and input. This may lead to a failure to focus on a few priorities in the context of the changed conditions in Europe and thus fail to achieve the corresponding efficiency at European level. These are measures that we now need to reform quickly. These are measures that should have been reformed long ago, and it will certainly not be an easy task to implement these reform measures in the light of the Austrian tradition that has developed in the research world. In my department alone there are the FFF, the FWF, the ITF, the TIG, the ASA, the BIT, the ARC and various other interests I have already mentioned. My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! The aim of this Federal Government is to use the 7 billion schilling, which are additionally pumped into research, in such a way that the leverage effect in the economy is as high as possible and that the efficiency of these funds also achieves a corresponding effect in the economy. With regard to that development, which is referred to as one-stop-shop, we believe that this will be a service to the economy in order to be able to hold on to the concert of the European Member States. The objectives of our structural measures are clearly defined: Creating a clear, customer-oriented and efficient structure, improving coordination and coordination between national programmes and international technology programmes, building up the necessary programme and institutional evaluation, clarifying the competence of FTI infrastructure participations and new priorities in cooperation with the federal states, harmonising the funding structures and processes, avoiding double-tracking, strengthening cooperation with the institutions of the federal states and setting up a funding compass. Project work is currently in progress. A large project group will meet tomorrow morning, consisting of various representatives of the research landscape and the ministerial institutions. We assume that, after this morning meeting, the key points for this reform will be to ensure that, in autumn, an appropriate legislative proposal can be introduced into Parliament and the objectives I have spoken about can also be legislately anchored. ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-11-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00111_d7e15443	Mr President. Federal Minister! High house! The Heads of State or Government of all EU countries set themselves a new strategic goal in Lisbon in March 2000: The European Union is to become the most competitive, knowledge-based economic area in the world by 2010 – an economic area capable of achieving sustainable economic growth with more and better jobs and creating a European research and innovation area. Ladies and gentlemen! The Austrian Federal Government has recognised this important objective when it takes office in 1999 and has set itself the goal of increasing the research rate to 2.5 percent of GDP by 2005. The starting point was that the research rate was only 1.79 percent in 1998, it was already 1.91 percent in 2001, and 1.95% of GDP is planned for 2002. You see very well the good development. One thing is also mentioned: At the start of office, Austria was clearly below the EU average, which is the result of the research rate. We have picked up this and are now finally at the EU average, so to speak, what the research rate is going to be. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] However, this federal government has not only recognized that Austria really needs to catch up and was a follow-up, but also looks into the future, works forward-oriented because it wants to guarantee prosperity and social security even in the future. I come back once again to the last thoughts of sustainability when we discussed the Gas Economics Act and the Ecostrom Act. Ladies and gentlemen! We should really succeed in decoupling the economic growth of the energy and resource consumption we have. I would remind you that if we don't change anything, the world's oil reserves will last only more than 40 years. I think many of us are about 40 years old and hope to live another 40 years. If you let this go through your head, you know how dramatic this situation is. We still want economic growth, and we need it, but that is, we need to decouple it from energy and resource consumption. Then it becomes clear that this will only be possible through new products with higher value added and on the other hand by creating new technologies in order to continue to provide products to consumers at attractive prices. Ladies and gentlemen! Research and development are not self-evident. They are necessary to secure the future. We need three things to enable research and development: On the one hand, we need money, we need the best minds, and we need clear strategies and priorities. I believe, ladies and gentlemen, that this Federal Government has done exactly these three important things for research and development. To the point money: An additional 7 billion Schilling, 509 million €, were made available for an offensive programme in research and development. Of course, it is extremely important to finance connections because research and development projects are multi-annual and long-term projects. It is also important that the economy is encouraged to invest more so that this leverage effect of 1 : 2 – a part comes from the public sector, two parts come from the economy – can actually be achieved. It was also important for the economic programme presented in December 2001, in which research grants were increased by plus 10 percent for the economy. New opportunities for additional research grants have been created, and a research premium has been introduced for the first time for small enterprises or for companies that do not make profits. It is also very gratifying that Governor Liebscher of the Austrian National Bank announced in a press conference that he is willing to devote 20 percent of the dividend of the Austrian National Bank for Research. Second point: We need the best heads in the country. It is gratifying that on the one hand, the development of professional universities is progressing very positively. In these colleges, proper priorities are set, also by the installed High School Council, which is chaired by a very competent businessman in the person of Mr. Raidl. On the other hand, it is also important that the technical universities offer very good networking between science and business and allow for a shorter study and thus a faster use of skilled staff. The university reform, which has been decided today, is also important. I would also like to thank Minister Gehrer for this important step towards university autonomy and the better possibility of cooperation between university research and the economy. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] I would like to mention the integration package, which also facilitates and simplifies the addition of key workers. The third point: Strategy and priorities. Ladies and gentlemen! I think it was very important that this federal government in this fragmented research landscape established a Council for Research and Technology Development, which also includes experts and economic operators – with a very profound expert in the scene as chairman, Knut Konsemüller. This Council for Research and Technology Development has already presented a research strategy for Austria in 2001 and will prepare and present a national research and innovation plan in September of this year, which will also define priorities and priorities where the research funds are to flow. I also think it is very positive that Minister Reichhold intends to give this Council for Research and Technology Development a legal personality and thereby make it more independent or independent. The cooperation between Federal Ministers Bartenstein and Grasser in Research Austria has already been mentioned positively, where access to research funding is to be bundled and simplified. Also important is mapping, i.e. an evaluation of the research funds in order to be able to produce an impact analysis in order to measure how efficiently the research resources are used and, so to speak, to actually determine the output. But I would also like to mention the FFF as a special point of contact for small and medium-sized enterprises. For many years now, economic research has been implemented very intensively. There is also a great leverage effect, that is, everything that flows over the FFF is certainly implemented by the economy in the desired ratio 2 : 1. Finally, I would like to address a delicate issue, namely biotechnology. Austria is being given a very good witness to what is happening to the site of biotechnology because we have MBAs – Krems is here to mention in relation to biotechnology – because we have and will have scientific specialists, especially through the Easter expansion, because we have a large number of patent applications, because we have large pharmaceutical companies that are interested in the location of Austria, and because we have public funding of research projects, such as the GEN-AU project. Ladies and gentlemen! This sector, this sector accounts for €2.5 billion and will create 12 000 new jobs by 2015. That is to say, it is important to continue developing in this area and to implement the European Biopatent Directive soon, as recommended by the Ethics Commission or the Research and Technology Development Council. Ladies and gentlemen! This Federal Government ensures that sustainable economies are made possible step by step, thus ensuring prosperity and social security in the future.

ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-11-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00111_d7e17752	Mr President. High house! I am happy to give a statement, in accordance with the will formation in the presidential conference and also in order not to endanger the timetable of the House before the summer break, on the restoration of the independence of our country, on the historical succession and on its assessment. Ladies and gentlemen! ==References== April 1945 "State Chancellor" Dr. Karl Renner – who, of course, was not a State Chancellor at the time, but a grey statesman –, brought back from Gloggnitz, began his first negotiations and talks with the Soviet Red Army. ==References====External links== Karl Renner then convened 40 members of the Provisional State Government in the municipal council hall of the Vienna City Hall on April 4, 1945. There the proclamation, then the Declaration of Independence and the Government Declaration of this new independent Republic were decided. The text is touching: It is an archaic language; sometimes it sounds a little old-fashioned, but it goes directly to the hearts and minds of the people it is about. [[President Dipl.-Ing. Prinzhorn is taking over the presidency again.]] I can present you with a few sentences. In the proclamation it says: "In view of the fact that the following of 1938 is not, as is natural between two sovereign states... agreed by negotiations... and concluded by state agreements, but initiated by military threat from the outside and the highly treasonous terror of a Nazi minority, listed a defenseless government leadership, finally forced by military military occupation of the country to the helpless people of Austria, in view of the... fact that Adolf Hitler "has led the powerless and willless nation of Austria into a senseless war of conquest, which no Austrian ever wanted to see fore or well-founded, for the war of peoples, against which no true Austrians ever put up feelings of enmity or hatred,..., many hundreds of thousands of the sons of our country, almost the whole of the youth..., has ever wanted to see ahead of it, or to do well, "the war of nations against it" is "... and the United Nations has been forced to do so" the United Nations, the United Nations has been forced to "union" the United Nations, the United Nations, the United Nations, the United Nations has been forced to "union", In view of this, the Declaration of Independence is proclaimed, and then it is referred to very honestly that, in addition to the Moscow Conference, Austria's co-responsibility "for participation... on the part of Hitler-Germany" is also mentioned, and "that the final regulation will inevitably take into account its own contribution to its liberation". Then comes the explanation: "Get up! Work together for all our liberation! Helps to rebuild the former independent community of the Republic of Austria!" And on top of that: "Do not forget that these first steps have only been made possible by the Red Army having redeemed large parts of our state territory from the pressure of the Hitler Army." And then also the confession of this new free republic is expressed that it wants to regain the confidence of the three world powers and the entire state world and the Republic – literally! – to return "to the ranks of sovereign states". "She will make an effort to... To conclude peace and friendship agreements, but above all with its immediate neighbours, with whom the Austrian people – despite all the political confusion of the past – worked and lived in the exchange of economic and cultural goods for many centuries." Since then, ladies and gentlemen, the Republic, the respective Federal Government, remembers on 27 May. April the independence of the Second Republic, the resurgence of an independent Austria. This is a fundamental consensus, one that has held, as the ÖVP with the SPÖ or SPÖ with the ÖVP, the Social Democrats with the Freeman or now we, the ÖVP with the Freeman, have formed a government. ==References== On April 1, 1955, Julius Raab, after returning from the negotiations in Moscow, gave a significant speech – the actual speech of the State Treaty before the Treaty was signed. It must be remembered that this declaration of independence was decided at a time when Austria was not yet free when this new Austria was not recognized; only the Soviet side, the Federation, recognized the Republic of Austria. ==References== On May 6, 1945, American troops liberated Innsbruck and Salzburg. ==References====External links== ==References====External links==* Official website Julius Raab recalls the nightly negotiations in the Wenzgasse in Hietzing in 1955 when it was tried to bring together a wealth of laws in order to ultimately enable the restoration of Austria, the reconstruction of its administration and its economy. Therefore, each year we have a certain sequence: ==References== April, on which the Council of Ministers is meeting and commemorating. ==References== May, in symbol of the liberation of an Austrian concentration camp, Mauthausen. This is an important anniversary for all of us. ==References== May, on which the German army capitulated and people throughout Europe, I think many stones have fallen from the heart because the war has ended. ==References====External links== May, who brought Austria the full restoration of its sovereignty and all the freedom. That's a whole thing: There can't be a part broken out. It cannot be calculated on or against. This is – I repeat – a whole thing. It's important that we go every year on this 27. April not only commemorate the restored independent Austria, but also the victim – and the victims are numerous. We remember the 580,000 Austrians who lost their lives – 65,000 Austrian Jews murdered, 35,000 civilian political victims, they were executed, they died in concentration camps and prisons – and we also remember the 380,000 deceased or prisoners of war, as well as the 100,000 dead who had to live in war or air war. This number is much more than just a number: That's where fates are! And it is a wound that does not heal. It is a wound that needs to be cared for, to be cleaned – the healing may be achieved by granting forgiveness to the victims. But above all, it must be remembered, and no Austrian and no Austrian should be excluded from this memory and from this holistic representation – but no one should be excluded from this commemoration on 27 May. ==References== ==References== May, when these anniversarys are yearning. It is crucial that one sees the whole thing in the rework of history. A few editorials will appear again tomorrow, in which I think some very serious errors will be described. For example, an error is that it means: Austria has not denacated; Austria has been denazated from outside! I ask you, ladies and gentlemen, the High House: Is this fair and fair in view of the founders of this Republic, who took place on 27 July. April 1945 – and in some cases even a few days before – have been in the death cell, including several of the founders of my political party and politicians of your political groups? I think it's important that you already see and recognize that It was done on its own that, for example, the West, Innsbruck, was freely handed over to the Americans by the power of the resistance movement. The fairness therefore requires that the own contribution of this new Austria also be incorporated into this holistic view. Allow me, ladies and gentlemen, to mention here also the objective facts that Hubert Feichtlbauer, for example, has brought together very touching and impressively. In fact, Austria has not simply stolen from history. That would not have happened either, not even been tolerated: neither from the inside nor from the outside. There were 136 829 preliminary investigations, more than 28 000 charges against Nazi sympathizers or perpetrators. There were 13 667 convictions, 30 death sentences were executed, which reached the proportion of the population: more per capita than in the Federal Republic of Germany. A quarter of all government officials lost their position in Austria because of their NS sewing, a large part of them suffered significant loss of income. The 100 000 entrepreneurs were regulated in accordance with the Economic Cleaning Act. And after 1945 there were a number of restitutions – certainly in many cases too late, half-hearted and not with the right tone. I would also like to point out that there have been many laws relating to reparations which have brought fifty billion schilling attempts on reparation attempts since 1945. The National Fund was set up, art treasures were restored, and it became – I thank you for that too! – thanks to the unanimous decision-making in this legislative period, two funds for forced labour from Central and Eastern Europe and the General Settlement Fund for Jewish victims and for refund gaps, i.e. for claims that have not been fulfilled in the past. I therefore ask that, when assessing what Austria is all about, what Austria has done or has failed to involve this whole range of elements. It is also not true that, as the author of a morning editorial claims, there is no revision of the history from 1945 to 1955 at all. We all know how it has been: Austria has not been free in the full state-law sense in 1945 – and crew is always something bitter! It had its reasons why Julius Raab also expressed in 1955 the longing of the Austrian people, the pressing impatience after the production of full, comprehensive freedom. And it had its reason that the state opera was re-opened with "Fidelio" and this was also directly related to this complete restoration of sovereignty and freedom. If you can read tomorrow how Franz Kreutzer, who has hired as a young gymnasiast in 1947 at the "worker's newspaper" – he has still come to the legendary Oskar Pollack with the fresh Matura certificate – and was hired there as a young reporter, as so-called Russian reporter who had the job – and that deserves respect! – to report every day, in the then difficult situation, the attacks that have happened – "Wildost in Vienna", "Human Robbery on the Light Day" – then there are facts that are only too well aware of the person concerned. And we know exactly – each one of us has acquaintances or family members who have experienced this time, or he has or has heard of acquaintances – what has happened. It was wrong! The government at that time also knew that, and it had to use a large part of its work and energy to intercede again and again, to ask for reparations, to ensure or to allow for freedom. A wealth of things not so well known in the public has been set there, things that have now been prepared. However, it does not need to be called in the current situation, because I myself, as Foreign Minister and now also as Federal Chancellor with President Vladimir Putin at the beginning of a state visit to Moscow, have completed the topic of the rehabilitation of Austrians which I started and started. Do not forget that tens of thousands of Austrians have suffered bitter injustice in the horror of Stalinism, who have practically been deprived of their civil dignity, their rights, and who have now experienced reparation and a restoration of their honor with this first rehabilitation by the new democratic system in Russia. I believe it is also important and important to acknowledge that it was possible with today's Russian leadership to set up a study project, "The Red Army 1945 to 1955", involving the most important Austrian scientists, so that this time can also be prepared. Ladies and gentlemen! I am therefore saying this so that we are aware of the issues, including the taboo issues, of old times, because it was not always easy to talk about them: It could be misunderstanded, it could be misinterpreted; some victims, as so often, have cheated on talking about it. And it is good that the veil of the past, of disposing, is torn away, that nothing is turned under the carpet – but that what is, is also pronounced. It is not that one can compare or link about the seven years of Nazi terror with the ten years of Russian or other occupation. There is no question at all for me that a completely different situation was given by the restoration of Parliament, Constitution, democracy in 1945. Therefore, I also ask you to understand that, in this time, we must put together the importance that there can be no comparisons, no calculation of the victims, no playing out of the feelings of those who were wrong before 1945, or of those who were wronged after 1945. And it is not an Austrian invention, but it is the truth that many thousands of Austrians had to go to concentration camps because of their resistance to National Socialism. But it is also the fact that many thousands of Austrians bear moral responsibility for the crimes of National Socialism, ladies and gentlemen. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Greens.]] That is why, ladies and gentlemen, this Europe needs this new vision, which is based on the ruins of the Second World War, so that this Europe can be more than just an economic union and thus, in fact, a new vision of peace and freedom. Ladies and gentlemen! Let me conclude by saying: Let us confess to these historical truths together! Let us not try to capture the history of party politics! Those who believe in a monopoly on political morality when working on and managing our history damage the basic democratic consensus in a totally irresponsible way. Let us therefore take this debate on the occasion of a renewed and future commitment to this basic democratic consensus.

ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-11-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00111_d7e23980	Mr President. Mr President, ladies and gentlemen! This new law creates clear responsibilities, there is a clear responsibility, there is basically what we are seeking from the government in many areas, especially for people with disabilities, who often have lack of mobility, the so-called one-desk principle, to say that in New German English. The section has worked for a very long time, it has worked very hard, and it has achieved good success. ParlaMint-AT_2002-07-11-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00111_d7e26428	Mr President. Minister! My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! In this debate, I would simply like to focus on one point, namely family hospice. I find it – and I have told you this, Mr Minister – really immense that we are now funding a very classic family benefit from the care allowance, although we all know that it is alleged that – as we are told again and again – there can be no valorization and will be, because the money is simply not there. But now, suddenly, a performance is being financed, although there is no money? - I wonder how this is going. Minister! If, for the financing of family hospice, the law now provides that people in need of care will receive grants when family hospice members do, then you also need to know that with it you exclude many people who are in the dying phase and do not have any family members from this performance. They also exclude people who die, for example, in stationary facilities where members would like to be there and where in practice it is not possible, however, because there is no funding for death support if someone is in the stationary area – not even a basic funding. This means that in the future only those people who have the financial background to be able to afford to leave the work process for six weeks or six months will be able to do so. Minister! That's not how it was supposed. It was also not thought that family members would suddenly be there with 2 000 s and live a month of it, because this is the average funding someone gets when he makes family hospice. Minister! I would like to tell you something from my professional experience before I enter Parliament so that you can see what such a situation really looks like: Imagine someone coming to the hospital today because he suffered a stroke. If he is released home in the death phase, it will not be enough if he receives care level 3, 4 or perhaps 5 as advance performance and a family member is to take over the death accompaniment. This care money is quite simply consumed by the outpatient service that is necessary for him in his situation. What then remains for the caring family? – The caregiver has virtually two possibilities: either to use an outpatient care service – then no more money remains for him to accompany his family – or to give up the outpatient service and to perform the service himself – but he will not be able to do so because he simply has no professional training available to him when he is active in outpatient care. Minister! In this dilemma you bring people with disabilities as well as their families. You have to think about that. This is not a solution to this problem. We need a solution that will ensure that, when someone takes on death accompaniment, it doesn't matter if he's a relative or a friend, and that the person providing death accompaniment knows in advance how much money she has available. Only in this way can the consideration be made of whether one can temporarily leave the profession in order to provide this care. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] Minister! We have talked about this, and you have told me – and I have believed you too – that you know that with this regulation exactly this type of scheme, this issue opens up. I therefore do not understand at all why a law is passed which in practice does nothing if one knows the problem and knows that this form of financing does not ensure that family hospice can really be done. I would have expected – and I also asked you – that we should put together once again and try to achieve a financing with clear rules, so that both people who are dying and their relatives who want to provide death support know what the situation looks like for them. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] Because I have a great sense of responsibility to disabled, old and dying people with whom I have worked for years and do not want them to die under stress, I cannot accept this bill. This, Mr Minister, would help to increase the fear of the dying. - I don't want to.
